| Symbolic calculation (الحساب الرمزي) defined — working with letters, each standing for a number; the opposite of a sum is the sum of the opposites, and the opposite of a difference: −(a − b) = b − a; removing brackets preceded by + (contents unchanged) and by − (write the opposite), built from money-balance situations; simplifying (اختزال عبارة رمزية) means the fewest terms — remove brackets, then group and collect the terms of each letter; multiplying two single terms (numbers then letters); expanding (a + b)(c + d) = ac + ad + bc + bd, introduced as the area of a rectangle split into four — an equality true for every value of the letters is an identity (مطابقة); expanding products with minus signs by rewriting subtraction as adding the opposite; testing a claimed symbolic equality by substitution — one value that fails disproves it outright, while agreement at several values only increases confidence; mental calculation (الحساب الذهني) and by-hand calculation named and distinguished | Syria, Grade 8 Algebra student's book, printed pp. 9–11, 19, 46–60 |
| History in the Unit 5 opener: al-Khwarizmi (c. 780–c. 850) and his book al-Kitab al-Mukhtasar fi Hisab al-Jabr wa'l-Muqabala — al-jabr, setting an equation right by completing it, and al-muqabala, balancing the two sides — the origin of the word algebra; expanding by the distributive law, k(a + b) = ka + kb and (a + b)(c + d) = ac + ad + bc + bd, introduced through rectangle areas (worked: (2x − 3)(4x − 1) = 8x² − 14x + 3); factorising by a common factor (x² − 3x = x(x − 3)) and by a common binomial factor; the famous identities stated and proved geometrically by areas — (a + b)² = a² + 2ab + b², (a − b)² = a² − 2ab + b², (a + b)(a − b) = a² − b² — expanded with, and factorised in reverse (x² + 6x + 9 = (x + 3)²; 16x² − 9 = (4x + 3)(4x − 3)); a find-the-error exercise in which the book plants a wrong expansion of (2x − 1)(3x − 2) as 6x² − 5x − 3 for pupils to catch — the correct expansion is 6x² − 7x + 2; identities used with surds to prove a number is an integer ((3 − 4√5)(3 + 4√5) = −71) | Syria, Grade 9 Algebra student's book, printed pp. 27, 32–37, 40 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 65 countries (Kazakhstan, Jordan, Armenia, Greece, South Africa, Egypt, Libya, Vietnam, Ghana, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, North Macedonia, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Turkey, Tanzania, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Australia, Brazil, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Bulgaria, Eritrea, Chile, Finland, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Gabon, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Italy)**.**.** Nepal carries expressions from missing-addend addition as its 'algebra' in Grade 2 English (L24, p. 91), through trial-method missing-number work in Grade 3 (L29, pp. 114–116), variable/coefficient/substitution with monomial/binomial/trinomial and like/unlike terms in Grade 4 (L9, pp. 116–130), algebraic expressions with like terms in Grade 5 (Unit 18, pp. 120–136), +/−/×/÷ of monomials and evaluation in Grade 6 (पाठ 12, pp. 131–145), polynomials with degree, binomial products and identities in Grade 7 (Unit 22, pp. 178–196), to factorisation, HCF/LCM of expressions and rational expressions in Grade 8 (Unit 20, pp. 161–187). The Grade 3 Nepali trial lines are written as false equations ('15 + 1 = 19') — the 2074 English edition's corrected wording is used; the Grade 5 book's generic variable symbols print garbled as `"`, `△`, `*` and are taught with letters. Bangladesh carries expressions from true and false mathematical sentences and open sentences with an unknown □, solved by trial and by the inverse operation relation, in Class 4 (Ch. 6 §§6.2–6.3, pp. 68–72) — stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep — through open sentences vs closed statements and solving problems using letters (relations of the y = 6x + 8 type) in Class 5 (Ch. 4, pp. 21–25), variables, operation symbols, expression/term/coefficient, factors of a term and like/unlike terms with addition and subtraction of expressions in Class 6 (Ch. 7, pp. 149–167) with the (a + b)² area analysis by paper cutting (Ch. 9, p. 179), through exponents of algebraic expressions and factorisation with HCF/LCM of algebraic expressions in Class 7 (Exp. 2, pp. 30–56; Exp. 9, pp. 181–190) and algebraic fractions with polynomial division in Class 7 (Exp. 11, pp. 215–226), binomial and trinomial expressions using solids — (a+b)³, (a−b)³, cube of a trinomial, factoring cubes — in Class 8 (Exp. 3, pp. 47–70), to polynomial expressions in Class 9 (Ch. 4, pp. 81–112) — forming polynomials, terms/variables/coefficients/degree, operations, remainder and factor theorems, factorisation and partial fractions. Book errors in this band are taught corrected: Class 7 p. 220 gives (21y²z²)/(−4y²z) as 3z (correct −5.25z); Class 9 English p. 85 calls the vertical line the x-axis (it is the y-axis), p. 86 says to join polynomial points with a straight line (Bangla: a smooth curve — only linear polynomials give a straight line), p. 93 says “xᵐyⁿ is the coefficient of a” (a is the coefficient of xᵐyⁿ), and p. 100's third bullet pairs x = −2 with divisor x + 1 (it goes with x + 2, remainder p(−2) = −2) — all English-only, Bangla correct; the p. 100 proof's divisor/quotient label-swap is a Bangla-original error carried into English; and the degree-of-a-term wording on p. 83 is garbled (correct: the sum of the exponents of the variables in the term), p. 102 “if only p(a) = 0” should read “if and only if”, and the pp. 108/111 claim of no common factor when degree of p(x) < degree of q(x) is false as a general statement. Iraq carries expressions from variables and algebraic expressions with substitution in Grade 6 (Ch. 2 L2–L3, pp. 37–40), through algebraic expressions and polynomials — terms, like terms, multiplying terms and numerical value — in Grade 7 (Ch. 1 L1-3, p. 16; Ch. 3, pp. 59–80), to polynomials in Grade 8 (Ch. 3, pp. 50–72) and algebraic expressions with factoring in Grade 9 (Ch. 2, pp. 27–53). Kazakhstan carries the fullest treatment — brackets and coefficients in Grade 6, then monomials, polynomials and the abridged-multiplication formulas in Grade 7. Jordan carries terms and expressions in Grade 7. Armenia carries letter expressions, monomials and polynomials in Grade 7. Greece carries the whole expressions chapter in Grade 9, including the notable identities. South Africa carries expressions as worksheet practice across Grades 8 and 9, from vocabulary to binomial products to factorising. Egypt carries expressions from numerical and algebraic expressions in Primary 5 (Discover) through expressions and patterns in Prep 1's new curriculum, expressions in Grade 6 and the four factorisation methods in Grade 8 (the Primary and Prep rows were completed by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep). Libya opens Grade 9 with expansions and factorisation. Vietnam carries one-variable polynomials in Grade 7 and multi-variable polynomials with identities and factorisation in Grade 8. Ghana carries only teacher-manual evidence (JICA Basic 5, Lessons 79–85). Mauritania’s 1AS teaches no algebra; its later algebra — calcul littéral in 2AS Ch. 4, 3AS Ch. 3 and 4AS Ch. 4, polynômes in 5AS — is stocked in the table above at the chapter pages the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep pinned (an earlier version of this note said Mauritania was not in this lesson). Laos carries expressions from recognising and expanding products in Grade 7, through notable products and factorisation in Grade 8, to polynomials and rational expressions in Grade 11. Yemen carries expressions from first symbolic expressions and substitution in Grade 6, through algebraic terms and expressions in Grade 7, to multiplying, dividing and factorising expressions and polynomials in Grades 8–10. Indonesia carries expressions from algebraic forms — variables, terms, coefficients, constants, the distributive property, factoring and like terms — in Grade 7 (Merdeka and K13 editions), through open and closed sentences in the Grade 8 Merdeka student book, to polynomials with division, factors, zeros and identities in the Grade 11 Tingkat Lanjut books (2021 and the 2024 Revisi edition). Nicaragua carries expressions from algebraic expressions with like terms in Grade 7, through polynomial operations in the Grade 8 student book, to the Grade 8 module's introduction to algebra and polynomial operations, and its notable products, factorisation and algebraic fractions (module evidence), and notable products with factorisation in Grade 9 (cubes only as Desafío material there). Ethiopia carries expressions from working with variables in Grade 5 — growing patterns generalised with variables, expressions and terms, verbal phrases as expressions, collecting like terms and substitution — to algebraic expressions and terms with simplifying and writing expressions and equations in Grade 7. South Sudan carries expressions from like and unlike terms with collecting like terms and forming and simplifying expressions in Grade 4, through collecting like terms and expanding brackets in Grade 6, simplifying expressions, substitution and forming equations in Grade 7, simplifying expressions and word statements into expressions in Grade 8, to simplifying expressions, substitution, expansion and factorising in Secondary 1 and algebraic expressions with expansion by area models and quadratic expressions in Secondary 2. Honest limits: Grade 4 p. 77 changes names mid-example (Kariem/Rachael → Mary/Peter) — cite the method, not the names; Grade 7 p. 54 Example 4 substitution is wrong (y computed as 12 instead of 7; the answer 362 is wrong, correct 163⅔) and is never cited. Cambodia carries expressions whole from letters for numbers in Grade 6 through identities, factorisation and the binomial theorem in Grades 8–11. Kuwait carries expressions from the variable concept introduced in Grade 6, through variables, expressions and evaluation in Grade 7, to polynomials and factorising by GCF and difference of two squares in Grade 8. Band C adds Grade 9 Term 1 factorisation — cubes, trinomials, perfect squares and four-term polynomials — and rational expressions (simplifying, multiplying, dividing, adding and subtracting) in Unit 3, with the book's two factorisation slips on p. 72 and its rational-multiplication slip on p. 125 taught corrected. North Macedonia carries expressions at the foundations in Grade 8 (2010 edition) — equality, equation and identity distinguished, the domain of an equation, and equations sorted by unknowns and by degree. Iran builds expressions from Grade 7 algebra through common-factor factorising in Grade 8 to the standard identities and rational expressions in Grade 9.

China carries expressions in the Grade 4 laws-in-letters work, the Grade 6 review pattern expressions, and factorising over the complex numbers in the senior A-edition Volume 2.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries expressions from Class 6's letters-for-numbers through Classes 7–8's polynomial arithmetic and identities to Class 9's factorisation types, HCF and LCM of expressions, and the remainder and factor theorems again in Class 11.

Syria introduces expressions in Grade 6, works terms, like terms and evaluation in Grade 7, gives symbolic calculation its own unit in Grade 8 Algebra — bracket removal as opposites, simplification to the fewest terms, expanding from rectangle areas, and a claimed identity disproved by one counterexample — and completes expanding, factorising and the three famous identities in Grade 9 Algebra, whose Unit 5 opens with al-Khwarizmi and the origin of the word algebra.

Saudi Arabia opens its Grade 4 patterns-and-algebra chapter with the expression itself — one expression shown three ways (drawing, words, numbers), then the clean distinction between a numerical expression and a numerical sentence.

Malaysia starts algebra in Form 1 (letters, terms, coefficients, like terms, adding and subtracting expressions) and continues in Form 2 with expansion and factorisation as a matched pair — expand one way, factorise back the other.

Rwanda teaches expressions in Primary 6: writing algebraic expressions from words in Unit 10, then collecting like terms and substituting values, including negative values, in Unit 11.

Sudan builds expressions from Grade 6 (variables, terms, like terms, evaluation), through Grade 7 open sentences, to Grade 9's full factorisation course — common factor, grouping, the square of a binomial, difference of two squares, sum and difference of cubes — and Grade 10's polynomials.

India carries expressions through its NCERT ladder — Mathematics Class 6–9 — including variables; forming expressions; building expressions; polynomials in one variable.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик I, II, V, VI, VII, VIII, IX, X, XII (Grades 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12): expressions from the first hidden-number boxes and triangle totals to the senior work with identities, factorising and rational expressions.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6), *Conciencia Matemática* (Grade 8) and *La Matemática y el Vivir Bien* (Grade 11). From its books: from Grade 6: algebraic language — Translating instruction sequences into algebraic language (m, 2.m, 2.m + 8, (2.m + 8)/2, m + 4 − m); from Grade 8: definition — polynomial function f(x) = aₙxⁿ + … + a₀ over Q; domain and codomain Q; variable vs indeterminate; from Grade 11: polynomial defined: degree, coefficients, ℤ[x], ℚ[x], ℝ[x], monic, leading coefficient, constant term; polynomial vs polynomial function.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 8: variable; algebraic expressions; evaluating an expression; simplifying algebraic expressions (like terms); multiplying monomial expressions; dividing monomial expressions; multiplying algebraic expressions (monomial by polynomial, area models); identities; square of the sum and square of the difference of two binomials; chapter key points and exercises (algebraic expressions); unknown and variable; from Grade 10: variables, constants, coefficients, like terms; algebraic expressions; polynomial vs rational vs irrational algebraic expressions; standard form of a polynomial; types of polynomials (monomial, binomial, trinomial); degree of a polynomial; zero polynomial; equal polynomials; ascending and descending polynomials; value of a polynomial; sum of coefficients of a polynomial; addition and subtraction of polynomials; multiplication of polynomials; special products / identities (a±b)², a³±b³.

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .2 Algebraic expressions and brackets: expanding brackets in formulas (plan 31). from Grade 9 (teacher's book): Expanding brackets; building and simplifying algebraic expressions; substituting values; Writing expressions and formulae; reading graphs/diagrams into formulae; Evaluating expressions for a given value; writing and using formulae (triangle formula situation). from Grade 10 (teacher's book): Substitution into and rearrangement of formulae (perimeter P = 2a + 2l worked). from Grade 11 (teacher's book): Transforming/rearranging formulae.

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 8: Expresiones algebraicas; Término algebraico; Términos semejantes; Clasificación de expresiones; Valor numérico; Signos de agrupación; Aplicación; Suma de monomios y polinomios; Resta de polinomios; Multiplicación; Polinomio por polinomio; División; from the Compendium's Secundaria 2 section (Grade 8): El álgebra y su relación con las actividades de la vida cotidiana; Operaciones con expresiones algebraicas; from the Compendium's Secundaria 3 section (Grade 9): Ecuaciones aplicadas al contexto y la tecnología; Productos y cocientes notables; from the Compendium's Secundaria 6 section (Grade 12): Álgebra y trigonometría preuniversitaria.

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Numerical expressions and the value of an expression; Letter (literal) expressions and their values; Convention for writing letter factors: number coefficient first, multiplication sign…; Simplifying letter expressions; Formulas: writing a rule with letters (M = 15t + 60) and using a formula; from Grade 6: Expanding brackets: a + before brackets keeps signs, a − before brackets flips every sign; Simple transformations of expressions: expanding brackets, collecting like terms, taking…; Mathematical language: its symbols (digits, =, >, <, ≥, ≤, %, brackets), Latin letters…; from Grade 7: Numerical expressions, their value, order of operations, expressions with no value…; Expressions with variables; Mathematical language: translating word statements into expressions and back; Identically equal expressions; Identity transformations: collecting like terms, opening brackets; Monomial (single term) and its standard form; Polynomial and its standard form; Adding and subtracting polynomials (bracket rules); Multiplying a monomial by a polynomial; Multiplying a polynomial by a polynomial; Square of a sum: (a + b)^2 = a^2 + 2ab + b^2, with mental-squaring applications; Square of a difference: (a - b)^2 = a^2 - 2ab + b^2; Applying the square-of-sum and square-of-difference formulas in simplification and…; Product of sum and difference: (a + b)(a - b) = a^2 - b^2; Cube of a sum and cube of a difference: (a ± b)^3 expansions; from Grade 8: Expressions and their values; Whole (integer) expressions vs rational expressions.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 1 syllabus requires pupils to use the vocabulary of the operations — sum, difference, product, quotient, add, subtract, divide, multiply, remainder — the arithmetic word-stock that this lesson's mathematical-language rows build on. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 3: a mathematician who left a mark: al-Khwarizmi (Muhammad ibn Musa) presented as the systematiser of algebra and the first to use zero in its present form, with his work in astronomy and geography.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: a distinctively concrete first algebra — a letter first stands for an object (k for a chicken, in family-counting stories that produce 4k) and only then is named a variable with its coefficient; like and unlike terms; adding and subtracting like terms by their coefficients; mixed simplification; multiplying and dividing algebraic terms.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Mathematics 6 (student); Algebra 7; Algebra 8 (Mikayelyan); Algebra 9; Algebra and Elements of Mathematical Analysis 10 (Arakelyan): opening brackets; identical transformations of letter expressions; monomial; degree of a monomial; like monomials; product of a monomial and a polynomial; sum and difference of polynomials; product of polynomials; square of a sum; square of a difference; difference of squares; sum of cubes; difference of cubes; cube of a sum and of a difference; rational expressions; polynomial in one variable; algebraic expressions.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 teacher-evidence item from 1 of the guides (Book 7; Grades 8).

**Book 7:** *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 2ème année du cycle secondaire collégial* (2AC = Grade 8, BIOF) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 808,839 bytes, 93 PDF pages. Items are the distinct topics of the 2AC distribution, printed p. 82 in the shared programme chapter read in this guide. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Literal calculation — simplifying and developing expressions (Al Moufid 2AC guide, 2AC distribution) | Morocco — *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 2AC, Grade 8* (teacher guide) — teacher evidence, 2AC distribution, printed p. 82 |

Morocco enters this lesson as teacher evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every book in Morocco's in-hand set is a teacher guide or teacher edition, not a pupil textbook, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as a pupil's book (the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent). Morocco's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 32 guides are read and stocked, but no pupil book is in hand.

Placements: liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-2026-11-10/maths/morocco-placements.md.

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 11 items (7 from student books, 4 teacher evidence) from Book S8, Book T7, Book S9, Book T8, Book S10, Book T9, Book S11, Book T10.

**Book S8:** *Libro de texto, 6.° grado* (student textbook, Grade 6, MINED 2026-tree edition) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); 21,048,663 bytes, 196 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. The two Vamos a Aprender books (Books 26–27) are image-only viewer assemblies with no text layer, read from rendered pages (covers, printed contents pp. 6–7 and body spot-checks — Matemáticas 10 printed p. 110 “Teorema del seno”, Matemáticas 11 printed p. 120 “Derivada de una función en un punto”); printed page = PDF page in the sampled range. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–27 in the placements' order. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Book errors: none material at placement grain — the Secundaria Activa Grade 9 module's contents page misprints two of its own headings (“Captítulo”, “Undiad 2”); the content is unaffected and rows use the corrected unit names. The placements' named exclusions — presentation letters, “how this book works” pages, author/imprint pages, transversal civics/health/environment themes, practice/evaluation apparatus (“Practica más”, “Evaluación del aprendizaje”, answer keys) and the Escuela Nueva “Estándares/Competencias” boxes at each unidad opening (MEN's own curriculum-standard statements; the taught content is placed at the guías themselves) — are stocked nowhere. The Círculos de Aprendizaje integrated books and the Retos para Gigantes Transición weekly guides are NOT in this set (filed as integrado, not matematicas) and are stocked nowhere. Colombia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (27/27) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 2 items from Book 18, Book 24.

**Book 18:** *Postprimaria — Matemáticas, Grado 8* (student guide) — Colombia, Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN); 20,256,968 bytes, 266 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages.

**Book 24:** *Secundaria Activa — Matemáticas, Grado 7* (student module) — Colombia, Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), Secundaria Activa; 174,397,469 bytes, 254 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages.

| Algebraic language: translating between ordinary and algebraic language | Colombia — *Secundaria Activa — Matemáticas, Grado 7* (student module), Grade 7, Cap. 1, printed pp. 159–163 |

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 17 teacher-evidence items from Book 2, Book 3, Book 4.

**Book 2:** *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 7–10) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 355/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); ISBN 978-99920-0-847-2; 1,808,872 bytes, 56 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 4:** *Programa de matemàtiques cientificotècniques del batxillerat general* (curriculum programme, Grades 11–12) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 242/2025 (BOPA Núm. 83, 9-7-2025); ISBN 978-99920-82-23-2; 4,325,201 bytes, 38 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). ## Angola added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. A Guia Prático Matemática 6ª exists only as an exact-link reader copy and is NOT in hand — cited nowhere; if one is ever secured it enters as teacher evidence only. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page bases: Books 1 and 3 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Books 2, 4, 5 and 6 — printed page = PDF page. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 item (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 6.

**Book 6:** *Matemática — Manual da 6.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 6) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-85-5; 2,544,284 bytes, 104 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book notes, taught corrected: printed p. 51 first presents the ambiguous expression 28 : 7 × 2 with two candidate readings and tells the pupil to ask the teacher — the rule is fixed by the worked examples on printed p. 52, and the course teaches the standard order (× and ÷ in order, before + and −); printed p. 62's direct-proportionality worked line prints the third ratio with denominator 6 where the sequence's term is 5 (150/5 = 30 — taught from the sequence). The book carries printer's proof marks, and one running footer misprints “Matemática 5ª Classe” (printed p. 99) — production matter, cited nowhere.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |

The old closed-52 inventory's “Moldova link-only, 0 in hand” is refuted: all 12 books sit secured in the tensae-code/liqlearns-textbooks GitHub repo and were read from there for this expansion (Grades 3, 4 and 6–12 via the mid-size-books-8 release; Grades 1, 2 and 5 via the Contents API on their textbooks.tsv git-blob paths), byte-verified — MD5s match books.json exactly for the ten Grades 3–12 books. ## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 9 requirement-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3 and Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 1–3* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,558,181 bytes, 263 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 51. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 4–6* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,278,639 bytes, 328 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 54. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 3:** *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* (examination syllabus) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Youth & Sports, Testing & Evaluation Section; 2,351,047 bytes, 10 PDF pages (a scanned-image PDF with no text layer, read visually page by page); printed page = PDF page − 1. The library records title this file “BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9” — the held file is the 2003 examination syllabus, and it is placed as what it is (set correction, stated here; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records). SYLLABUS EVIDENCE ONLY — an examination syllabus states what candidates are required to know; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 4:** *Senior High School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 10–12* (curriculum guidelines; the held file is the July 2019 revision, footer “High School Mathematics Curriculum – The Bahamas, 2019”) — Bahamas, Department of Education; 3,766,723 bytes, 219 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

| SUBSTITUTION — substituting a numeral in algebraic expressions and formulae, including powers and roots (Grade 10 requirement) | Bahamas — *Senior High School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 10–12* (curriculum guidelines, July 2019 revision) — curriculum evidence, printed p. 78 |
| BRACKETS (EXPANSION) — simplifying an expression by removing brackets, and finding the product of two binomial expressions using the distributive property and/or FOIL (Grade 10 requirement) | Bahamas — *Senior High School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 10–12* (curriculum guidelines, July 2019 revision) — curriculum evidence, printed pp. 78–79 |
| BRACKETS (EXPANSION) — calculating the product of expressions in two brackets each containing two terms, and squaring a binomial expression of the form (ax + b) (perfect squares and the difference of two squares in the Contents) (Grade 11 requirement) | Bahamas — *Senior High School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 10–12* (curriculum guidelines, July 2019 revision) — curriculum evidence, printed pp. 128–129 |

The Bahamas enters this lesson as curriculum evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every document in the Bahamas' in-hand set is a ministry requirement document (the 2010 primary guidelines, the 2003 BJC examination syllabus, the 2019 senior-high guidelines), not a pupil textbook or a teacher guide, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as teaching (the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent). The Bahamas' class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 in-hand documents are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand; it advances only when a pupil book file — a Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* volume, or the Akhepran Grade 7 BJC textbook whose exact link already sits in the download list — lands in hand and is read.

## Belize added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Belize's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 documents, all of them Belize Ministry of Education *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide* books (August 2012) — Infant 1, Standard 1 and Standard IV; 0 pupil (student) textbooks** — and placed 108 teaching items. Every document in the set is a teacher's guide: it states the learning outcomes pupils must reach and teaches the teacher how to teach each one (comments, sample lesson objectives, specific content, sample activities and assessment). Every Belize item in this section is therefore TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE — Belize's guides teach teachers how to teach the topic; no Belize pupil textbook is in hand — cited as the evidence kind it is; Belize is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until a Belize pupil book is in hand and read. No pupil Maths book is in hand: the pupil books on the official National Textbook Program list (*My BZ Math* Infant 1–Standard 4, Macmillan; *Caribbean Primary Math* Bk 6 for Standard 5, Hodder; *Active Math* for Standard 6, Macmillan) are all recorded missing in the library files, and the guides' own textbook linkages point to the legacy pupil series *Primary Mathematics for Belize* (Hodder, by term codes Y1T1…/S1T1…), which is named by the guides only — not in hand, not read, and not a course source. Three further Maths documents — the *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* division webs (Lower Division, Middle Division, Upper Division) — are recorded at exact link only, never downloaded and in no books.json; they are not part of this read and are cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered learning outcome in each guide's Standards and Learning Outcomes section (35 outcomes in each guide, 105 in all) plus 3 items taught in a detailed section but stated in no outcome of that guide (items 64, 65 and 87, marked D); outcome numbering is continuous across the series (Infant 1 carries M1.1–M20.1, Standard 1 continues at M1.10, Standard IV at M1.16), which is why the codes do not restart at 1 in the later guides. Printed page = PDF page in all three guides. The placements' named exclusions — the guides' front matter (programme philosophy: goals, rationale, assessment discussion), strand introductions and Big Ideas prose, sample lesson objectives, sample assessments, sample activities and print/technology resource columns as apparatus, the shared manipulatives list, the websites lists, the shared Mathematics Glossary (carried in the placements as record item R2 — a teacher reference list, cited nowhere as content), and the standards each guide marks N/A at its level (Book 1: M2 place value and M13 equations; Book 2: M14 measurement concepts; Book 3: M5 position/ranking numbers and M14) — are stocked nowhere. The 14 book errors and inconsistencies found in the read (placements E1–E14) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: item 35 is placed as data interpretation, not probability, though the guide files it under its probability standard (E5); item 51's set language is taught with “complement” for the book's “compliment” (E6); item 66's many-to-one correspondence is taught as one symbol or unit standing for many objects, not the guide's function-style definition (E8); item 82 is stocked WITHOUT the outcome's fourth bullet (“expand powers with same base to add or subtract”), which is not a valid add/subtract procedure (E9); item 81 is placed as stated and flagged as vague and advanced for its level (E10); item 104 is placed as measurement and taught with the standard measurement meanings, the guide's own definition of precision being a data-management sense (E13); and item 106 follows the guide's own correct definitions — its assessment text's “medium” for median is an error (E12). Into this lesson the placements stock 2 teacher-guide-evidence items from Book 1 and Book 2.

**Book 1:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Infant 1* (teacher guide, Grade 1) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 738,164 bytes, 58 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–52. Printed page = PDF page. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

**Book 2:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard 1* (teacher guide, Grade 3) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 803,827 bytes, 62 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–56. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series from Book 1 (M1.10 onwards). TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

## Australia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Australia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 books, all of them student textbooks** (Years 8–10: eight Cambridge volumes — the Essential Mathematics Australian Curriculum, Victorian Curriculum and CambridgeMATHS NSW lines, plus the GOLD support edition and the CORE pathway volume — and one Jacaranda volume, Maths Quest 10+10A) — and placed 844 teaching items. Australia has no government textbook series (ACARA writes the Australian Curriculum but publishes and prescribes no textbooks); the set is the school-used commercial series the country records name, and every book is cited as such. All 9 files are distinct works (distinct ISBNs, extents and contents, verified per book on the read); the publisher-line relations are stated in the placements: Book A1 (GOLD) is the support edition of Book A2's Year 8 material, Book A3 is the same line in its Victorian ©2024 edition, Books A4 and A6 are the NSW syllabus editions, and Book A9 is Book A8's CORE pathway companion. In the sections below the books are cited as Books A1–A9. Australia COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure: every item is pupil-textbook teaching. The read's four flagged claims are carried exactly: (F1) Book A3's contents lists its Chapter 11, Algorithmic thinking, as “(online only)”, but the file in hand contains the full chapter, bound after the Answers (printed pp. 926–941) — it is read and placed, and it founds this course's algorithmic-thinking lesson; (F2) Book A7's contents lists a Chapter 18, Algorithmic thinking, with no page numbers, and the chapter is NOT in the file (searched end to end; the book closes with its Glossary, printed p. 1106, and Index, printed p. 1117) — recorded as absent, not read, and no item rests on it; (F3) Book A9's running head at printed p. 644 prints section 10E's code as “0E” — the book's own production error; the placement cites 10E, as the section's heading and contents give it; (F4) only the 9 Maths books are this read's set — the country records' wider Australia picture (12 staged books across subjects, plus filled-by-exact-link titles) is not merged into this count, and in particular the two senior Maths titles that exist only as exact-link records (Cambridge Senior Specialist Mathematics, QCE Units 3&4, 2e, and Cambridge Senior Mathematical Methods, QCE Units 3&4, 2e) are NOT in hand and were NOT read. The placements' named exclusions — front matter and “how to use this book” guides; every chapter's pre-tests, progress quizzes, unlettered applications / problem-solving / modelling / investigation spreads, puzzles and games, chapter summaries, checklists and reviews, semester reviews, glossaries, indexes and answer sections — are apparatus/assessment, teach no new content, and are stocked nowhere. The set is Years 8–10 secondary only, so the primary lessons (counting, place value, first steps) receive no Australia item — that is the set's shape, not a reading gap. Australia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (9/9) has been read and placed; the two senior QCE exact-link titles do not block the in-hand class (if exact-link titles are ever ruled in-scope for the courses reckoning, Australia drops to PARTIAL until they are grabbed and read). Into this lesson the placements stock 42 items from Book A1, Book A2, Book A3, Book A4, Book A5, Book A6, Book A7, Book A8, Book A9.

## Brazil added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Brazil's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDF — **1 book, a pupil textbook: *Matemática — Livro Didático Público — Ensino Médio* (SEED-PR), the Paraná state public textbook for Ensino Médio (Grades 10–12), in Portuguese. There is no teacher guide in the Brazil Maths set in hand, so every Brazil row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Brazil counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 128 teaching items. The expansion map's “1 student + 0 teacher = 1” is correct: books.json (grade-10/gov/brazil) and textbooks.tsv each carry exactly one Brazil Mathematics entry, and phase3-push-state.json lists the file as pushed. The staging folder for the book is empty, so there was no local copy to read, and the authenticated GitHub asset route failed in this pass (the mid-size-books release's asset listing repeatedly disconnected through the proxy, and the asset's download URL answered 404) — the GitHub copy's existence and size were therefore NOT verified here, and a library-side check of that asset is owed (a route failure, not a negative finding). The book was instead read from its own recorded source_url — the Wayback capture banked by the 2026-10-22 CDX prep (capture 20240421222141) — which landed at exactly the prep's expected 6,379,928 B, with 240 pages and the correct cover and catalogue block; the book read is certainly this book. Printed page = PDF page throughout. Four book errors found in the read (placements E1–E4) are carried corrected on the rows they touch — in the sequences-and-series (E1), ratio-and-proportion (E2), coordinate-geometry (E3), barycentres (E4) and loci (E4) lessons; two further misprints are recorded, not taught (R5: the risk exponent typeset once as 0.2525 for 0.2528, printed p. 58 — noted on the item 28 row in the logarithms-and-exponentials lesson; R6: glucose printed “C6A12O6”, printed p. 35, a chemistry misprint), and the Aristarchus bound passage (printed p. 169, record item R7) could not be confirmed either way as extracted and is cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the four section introductions and the general presentation (framing and history), the context essays and debates that carry no new maths, the biographical and historical passages except where the passage itself teaches a method, the “Obras Consultadas” reference lists after every chapter, and the blank “Anotações” pages — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 2 items (all from the pupil textbook) from Book 1.

**Book 1:** *Matemática — Livro Didático Público — Ensino Médio* (pupil textbook, Ensino Médio — Grades 10–12) — Brazil, Secretaria de Estado da Educação do Paraná (SEED-PR), Curitiba, 2nd edition 2006, ISBN 85-85380-39-X; 6,379,928 bytes, 240 PDF pp., MD5 e3dac71a6d075dd142282376da6fa785. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Read from the banked Wayback capture of the book's recorded source_url at the exact recorded size; the GitHub release-asset copy could not be verified in this pass (route failure — a library-side asset check is owed). The catalogue block's “p. 216” is the 1st-edition pagination and does not match this file's 240 pages (record item R1); all citations use the actual printed folios. Folha 6's chapter heading (printed p. 77) adds “Corrupção & Política –” to the contents-list title (record item R2) — the same chapter. Folhas 9 and 10 are filed by the book inside its Funções section although their content is trigonometry (record item R3); the placements follow the content.

## Bhutan added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Bhutan's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **16 files: 13 student books and 3 teacher's guides of the Royal Government of Bhutan's *Understanding Mathematics* series (Department of School Education / Ministry of Education and Skills Development), English-medium throughout** — and placed 657 pupil teaching items and 30 teacher-evidence items. **Set correction, proved by the read: the expansion map's count of 10 student + 3 teacher is WRONG — the map counted only the textbooks.tsv snapshot (PP, I, II, IV–X) and missed the Class III, XI and XII student books recorded as secured and pushed in the staging books.json files and the push state; the true in-hand set is 13 student + 3 teacher = 16 files, every one grabbed and verified (byte size on every file; MD5 against books.json on the 11 files that carry one — all match; page counts match every record) and read in full.** The Class I staging-path collision is resolved: the main-branch grade-01 file is the student activity book (Book 3); the Class I teacher's guide (Book 4) is a separate, MD5-verified file that books.json records under the same staging path name. **Critical filing flag, carried exactly: both BHSEC filings claim "complete book, not a sample/preview" — FALSE. The Class XI file holds only chapters 1–5 of the 26 its own contents lists and the Class XII file only chapters 1–4 of the 19 its contents lists; only the chapters actually present were placed, and both books are cited as the partial excerpts they are.** The 3 teacher's guides are teacher evidence only: their items are named below as teacher evidence and are never counted as pupil teaching. The Class V textbook's Getting Started spreads, GAME and CONNECTIONS features, Unit Revisions, Glossary, Measurement Reference and Answers are apparatus/practice and are stocked nowhere. The Class VII textbook's Getting Started spreads, GAME and CONNECTIONS features, Unit Revisions, Glossary, Measurement Reference and Answers are apparatus/practice and are stocked nowhere. The Class VIII textbook's Getting Started spreads, GAME and CONNECTIONS features, Unit Revisions, Glossary, Measurement Reference and Answers are apparatus/practice and are stocked nowhere. Its contents misprints §5.3.2 (Surface Area of a Rectangular Prism) at printed p. 157, after the Unit 5 Revision at p. 150; the sequence places it at ~p. 147 and the row cites it so. ## Cabo Verde added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cabo Verde’s whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 books, all of them student (pupil) manuals of the Ministério da Educação de Cabo Verde: *Manual de Matemática 3º ano* (Edição 2019), *Manual de Matemática 5º ano* (Edição 2020) and *Matemática 7º ano — Manual do(a) aluno(a)* (Edição 2020); 0 teacher guides** — and placed 197 teaching items (Grade 3: 47, Grade 5: 69, Grade 7: 81; 391 PDF pages, every page read). The expansion map’s count of 3 student + 0 teacher is CORRECT. Set boundary, proved from the records: `textbooks.tsv` carries exactly 17 Cabo Verde rows (all Government, grades 1–7), of which exactly three are Mathematics, all student; the download list’s §C enumerates 93 ministry manuals, including Maths manuals for grades 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 9, 10 and 11 and applied-Maths manuals — every one of those is exact-link-only (no TSV row, no staging file, no GitHub copy) and NOT in hand, so none is cited here; counting any of them would be a set error. Record gap, reported not fixed (record-side): `phase3-push-state.json` contains no Cabo Verde entry at all, and there is no Cabo Verde folder or `books.json` in phase3-staging — the in-hand evidence is the TSV’s GitHub URLs themselves, and all three GitHub copies were located, verified (byte size and MD5; the Grade 5 ministry copy is byte-identical to the GitHub copy, so that book is one book, cited once) and read directly, so the set is settled despite the push-state gap. No duplicates exist in the set: three distinct grades, three distinct books. Printed page = PDF page in all three books. The placements’ named exclusions — front matter, unit openers, objectives lists and the closing national-anthem page in the Grade 5 and Grade 7 books; the Grade 3 book’s cover plate and welcome/mascot pages (PDF pp. 1, 3, 5), its part-opener pages (PDF pp. 55, 85, 107, 111), its closing test “Teste” (printed pp. 125–129 — mixed assessment re-using taught content, no new teaching) and the flag / national-anthem page (PDF p. 130) — and pure exercise / consolidation blocks that teach nothing new are stocked nowhere. The 12 book errors found in the read (2 in the Grade 5 book, 5 in the Grade 7 book, 5 in the Grade 3 book) are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 15 items from Book 2 and Book 3.

## Canada added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Canada's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **2 works in 4 files: *Math First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide, Elementary & Secondary* (FNESC/FNSA, ©2020), 273 pp. — the one book in Canada's classified in-hand Maths set (0 student + 1 teacher) — and the borderline pupil work *I Like Math!* (Government of Nunavut, 2017), one Level 8 guided-reading fiction book held in three language editions of 11 pp. each and filed in every record under the Inuktut Ilinniaqta Early Books label, not under Maths** — and placed 117 items (112 teacher-guide evidence items from the guide, items 1–112, plus 5 pupil items from *I Like Math!*, items S1–S5). Every item from the guide is TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE: the guide directs teachers how to teach BC mathematics through First Peoples contexts, with mini-units, activities and blackline masters, and states BC curriculum connections in its tables; it expressly does not explain the mathematics or the curriculum in detail (its printed p. 35) and presumes a trained teacher. No pupil Maths textbook is in hand for Canada: Canada has no national textbook series (education is provincial/territorial), and the five provincial student textbooks in the records (Nelson *Principles of Mathematics 10*; McGraw-Hill Ryerson *Principles of Mathematics 9*; Pearson *Math Makes Sense 3, 7, 8*) stand filled-by-exact-link only — never staged, never secured, not read here and cited nowhere (a sixth, *Nelson Mathematics 8*, is a candidate only, its imprint never read). Canada is therefore cited as EVIDENCE ONLY, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent: Canada is added to this lesson's countries line and named in its rows, but counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure. The *I Like Math!* items are stocked as pupil evidence from that work exactly as the placements label them — 3 carry taught content (S1, S2, S4) and 2 are context/named-only (S3, S5) — and are likewise counted in no taught-in figure; whether the Nunavut early-books titles count as pupil Maths at all is a record-side question for the parent, reported here, not ruled on. The guide's Water-Keepers items (98–105) rest on the guide's printed teaching alone: the separate FNESC Water Keepers digital resource the guide points to (printed p. 224) is in no record as in hand and was not read. The guide is the 2020 revision of the 2011 *Math First Peoples* resource; the 2011 predecessor located on a Padlet host is not a separate in-hand book and is cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the guide's introduction, goals/rationale and teacher-reflection material; First Peoples Principles of Learning, story protocols and respectful-practice rules; the resource-design rubric; community-visit apparatus beyond the Interview Guide's six content categories; unit-organization front matter; the cultural/historical context narratives and stories inside the mini-units (context-setting only; no story text is reproduced anywhere in the placements or here); BLM worksheets that merely repeat an itemised activity; formative/summative assessment prompts, guiding questions, video and website lists and the Additional Resources bibliographies; and the *I Like Math!* front/back matter — teach no new mathematics and are stocked nowhere. The 5 book errors found in the read (placements E1–E5) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: E1 (sphere-volume worked example, printed p. 123 — a unit slip and a garbled intermediate line inside a volume calculation; the final answer, 500 cm³, is correct) on item 56; E2 (BLM 6 conversion sheet, printed p. 185 — the percent-to-decimal rule states the decimal-point direction backwards; both printed examples are correct) on item 83; E3 (Moose Tracks answer key, printed p. 181 — mixes “less than or equal to 20” with m < 20 and marks the boundary value inconsistently) on item 71; E4 (Circle Unit Answer Key, printed p. 211 — says “smoke hole” for “fire circle”; the project instructions at printed p. 204 state it correctly) on item 92; E5 (Weaving BLM list, printed p. 55 — numbers both Keep or Toss sheets BLM 16; they are BLM 15 and BLM 16) is a list typo affecting no item's content. Printed page = PDF page in the guide throughout; *I Like Math!* citations are its printed story folios. Canada's class for Maths is **PARTIAL (evidence-only)**. Into this lesson the placements stock 5 items (items 27, 28, 29, 38 and 60).

**Book 1:** *Math First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide, Elementary & Secondary* (teacher resource guide) — Canada, First Nations Education Steering Committee (FNESC) and First Nations Schools Association (FNSA), ©2020; 32,264,979 bytes (byte-exact against the record), 273 PDF pages. Printed page = PDF page throughout. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide directs teachers how to teach BC mathematics through First Peoples contexts and states curriculum connections; it expressly does not explain the mathematics in detail (printed p. 35); it is not a pupil textbook.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |

| Generalising the chevron rule to 2c − 1: variable, coefficient, constant, expression vs equation, substituting c = 30 to get 59, and the convention of writing the coefficient before the variable | Canada — *Math First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide* (FNESC/FNSA, 2020) (teacher guide) — teacher-guide evidence, printed pp. 58–59 |
